| OffSec Cert | Exam | Prerequisites | Official Course | Lab Platform | Timeline |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OSCP | OSCP Written + OSCP 24-hr Practical | None | OffSec PEN-200 (Penetration Testing with Kali Linux) | Proving Grounds Practice/Play | P08 |
| OSEP | OSEP Practical | OSCP | OffSec WEB-200 (Web Security Professional) or OffSec PEN-201 | Proving Grounds Labs | P11–P12 |
| OSWE | OSWE Practical | OSCP + OSEP | OffSec WEB-300 (Advanced Web Exploitation) or OffSec PEN-207 | Proving Grounds Labs | P12 |
| OSED | OSED Practical | OSCP | OffSec EXP-301 (Exploit Development) or OffSec PEN-217 | Proving Grounds Labs | P14 |
| OSCE3 | OSCE3 Practical | OSCP + OSEP + OSED (stack requirement) | OffSec combines PEN-200 + PEN-207 + PEN-217 prep | Proving Grounds Practice | P14 |
| OSEE | OSEE Practical | OSCP + 10 yrs professional | OffSec WEB-600 (Advanced Web Security) | Proving Grounds Elite | P34+ (MIT era, optional) |

**Recommended supplements to official OffSec materials:**
- *The Hacker Playbook 3* — Peter Kim (additional attack scenarios, red team methodology)
- *Penetration Testing: A Hands-On Introduction to Hacking* — Georgia Weidman (foundational techniques)
- TryHackMe, HackTheBox practice platforms (additional labs parallel to OffSec labs)
- Exploit-DB, searchsploit, GitHub exploit repositories (for OSWE/OSED techniques)

## D11: SANS MSISE & ADVANCED CERTIFICATIONS
### P13–P21: SANS MSISE (Master of Science in Information Security Engineering)

The SANS MSISE is a graduate degree that bundles 10 GIAC certifications with formal coursework. Primary sources are the SANS course materials for each corresponding GIAC/course.

**GIAC Certs embedded in MSISE:**
1. GSEC (Securities Essentials)
2. GCIA (Network Intrusion Analyst)
3. GCIH (Incident Handler)
4. GSOC (Security Operations)
5. GREM (Reverse Engineering)
6. GXPN (Exploit Techniques)
7. GPEN (Penetration Tester)
8. GMOB (Mobile Security)
9. GLEG (Legal/Compliance)
10. GIWN (Industrial/IoT)

Each course has its primary SANS materials (lecture slides, books, labs) + no adequate third-party study guides. Index building is the key study technique across all 10 exam stacks.

# SECTION E — TIMELINE-KEYED STUDY ROADMAP

This section maps each reading list section to the period in the Master Timeline (P01–P69) where focused study should occur. The intention is to give clear guidance on WHEN to read, not just WHAT to read.

**P01–P08 (Ages 24–26): Foundation phases**
- **Primary:** Section A (Foundational Theory) — A1 Networking, A2 Linux/OS, A3 Windows, A4 Cloud intro
- **Certs:** CompTIA A+/Network+ (D2), CCNA (D1), GFACT/GSEC (D3)
- **Elective:** Section C (Military) Part 1 — Physical Readiness, Navigation, Basic Military qualification
- **Key texts from A:** Tanenbaum *Computer Networks*, Stevens *TCP/IP Illustrated*, Arpaci-Dusseau *Operating Systems: Three Easy Pieces*

**P09–P14 (Ages 26–28): Consolidation phases**
- **Primary:** Section A (Foundational Theory) — A5 Offensive Security, A6 Defensive Security, A7 Cryptography (first 3 topics)
- **Engineering:** Section B (PEO Electrical, Computer, Software) — to support Carleton BIT coursework
- **Certs:** CCNP Security intro (D1), OSCP (D4), GIAC multiple (D3), CompTIA CySA+/PenTest+ (D2)
- **Key texts:** Bejtlich *Practice of Network Security Monitoring*, Sikorski & Honig *Practical Malware Analysis*, Ferguson/Schneier *Cryptography Engineering*

**P15–P21 (Ages 28–31): Hardware + AI fusion phases**
- **Primary:** Section A — A9 Hardware/Embedded Systems, A10 AI/ML, A11 Robotics/Automation
- **Engineering:** Section B (PEO Electronics, Mechatronics) — for EET diploma coursework
- **Military:** Section C — Combat Tactics, Strategy, Field Operations (age 30+ staggered start)
- **Certs:** SANS MSISE coursework (10 GIAC certs bundled), Microsoft Power Platform (D6), AWS Solutions Architect (D7)
- **Key texts:** Horowitz & Hill *Art of Electronics*, Goodfellow et al. *Deep Learning*, Lynch & Park *Modern Robotics*

**P22–P33 (Ages 31–34): Research master's phase**
- **Primary:** Section A — A7 Pure Mathematics (full 30 books across 11 pure math domains), A8 Physics (18 books)
- **Research focus:** Academic reading aligned with UofT MASc thesis direction + MIT PhD admission preparation
- **Certs:** Light background — CFA Level I/II (D13), cloud provider architect certs if needed (D7)
- **Key texts:** Rudin *Principles of Mathematical Analysis*, Katz & Lindell *Introduction to Modern Cryptography*, Peskin & Schroeder *Intro to QFT*

**P34–P45 (Ages 34–41): MIT PhD phase**
- **Primary:** Section A — A8 Physics (deep study), A7 Cryptography theoretical (advanced), A12 Quantum Computing
- **Research:** Domain-specific deep dives aligned with advisor research group
- **Certs:** Intelligence/OSINT (SANS FOR508/578/589), CFA Level III (D13), MIT Executive certs
- **Tactical/Military:** Section C — Strategic Theory (age 35+), Intelligence domain deepens
- **Key texts:** Weinberg *Lectures on Quantum Mechanics*, Nielsen & Chuang *Quantum Computation & Information*, current research papers in advisor's field

**P46–P51 (Ages 41–46): Cisco + CompTIA Completion phase**
- **Primary:** Section D1 (Cisco 63-credential master roadmap) + D2 (CompTIA folded margins)
- **Certs:** All 8 CCNP tracks + 7 CCIE Expert labs + CCDE + Tech/Business (D1); CompTIA remaining (D2)
- **Aviation:** Transport Canada PPL/IR study materials (D13)
- **Key texts:** Official Cisco/CompTIA study guides per §D1–D2
- **Context:** Post-PhD, highest income, lowest academic-pressure window — ideal for credential acceleration

**P52–P59 (Ages 46–52): Stanford JD/MD phase**
- **Elective deep dives:** Section A (selected mathematics, cryptography foundations for later research)
- **Professional:** Section D7–D13 — Healthcare/Medical Device certs if pursuing MD track, FDA regulatory certs, health informatics
- **Military:** Section C (Demolitions age 50+) — Canadian Blasters License background reading
- **Key texts:** Medical Device Software Development (D13), FDA documentation, *Biomedical Informatics* (A13)

**P60–P68 (Ages 52–57): Harvard postdoc phase**
- **Research:** Disciplinary deep study aligned with Harvard research fellowship direction (health law, informatics, bioethics)
- **Optional:** Section C (full military theory for context, if tactical consulting remains interest)
- **Certs:** MIT Executive Education (COO, CPO, CSO) if pursuing parallel thought leadership (D6 Microsoft context)
- **Key texts:** Current research literature in health law, bioethics, health IT policy

**P69+ (Ages 57–65+): Mission state**
- **Refresh:** Section A (selective updates on AI/ML, Quantum Computing as fields evolve)
- **Specialization:** Deep reading in operational areas: Web3 security (auditing firms), PQC implementation, ZK privacy systems, D-Central governance
- **Reference:** All sections used as reference material — less structured study, more applied knowledge maintenance

2. The Mom Test — Rob Fitzpatrick
3. Continuous Discovery Habits — Teresa Torres
4. Crossing the Chasm — Geoffrey Moore
5. Obviously Awesome — April Dunford
6. Good Strategy Bad Strategy — Richard Rumelt
7. Principles of Corporate Finance — Brealey, Myers & Allen
8. Security Analysis — Graham & Dodd
9. Thinking, Fast and Slow — Daniel Kahneman
10. Deep Work — Cal Newport
11. The Pragmatic Programmer — Hunt & Thomas
12. On Writing Well — William Zinsser

# PART 2 — OFFICIAL PEO DISCIPLINE READING LISTS (14 disciplines, ~470 entries)

> See companion document `peo_official_reading_lists.md` for the complete extraction of all 14 official PEO Suggested Text Listings (Biomedical, Building, Chemical, Civil, Computer, Electrical, Geological, Industrial, Manufacturing, Mechanical, Mechatronics, Nuclear, Engineering Physics, Software), organized by exam subject code exactly as PEO publishes them. That document is appended here by reference rather than duplicated in full, to keep this file navigable — the two together form the single comprehensive reading canon.

---

*Master Reading List (12-per-topic expansion), combined with the official PEO reading lists, generated April 2026. Confidence is highest for entries 1–6 per topic across the board; entries 7–12 broaden coverage into more specialized or newer texts and should be verified for current edition/availability before purchase, consistent with the caveat applied throughout the PEO expansion.*



### §6.B.COMPLETE: PEO Engineering Discipline Canon (Complete)


# PEO OFFICIAL SUGGESTED TEXT LISTINGS — COMPLETE EXTRACTION
## Redji Jean Baptiste (Toussaint) — All 14 Discipline Reading Lists, Every Book
### Source: official PEO "Suggested Text Listing" documents, per discipline, per exam subject code

---

> **Correction from the prior version of this document:** PEO *does* publish reading lists — one per engineering discipline, organized by exam subject code (e.g., `16-Elec-A1`), used for the confirmatory academic-requirements exam route. The earlier claim that "PEO does not publish a reading list" was wrong. These 14 lists are the authentic source documents, extracted in full below, code by code, exactly as PEO lists them ("please feel free to use the most recent edition" applies throughout).
>
> **Coverage:** Biomedical/Biochemical (2004) · Building (2007) · Chemical (2016) · Civil (2016) · Computer (2017) · Electrical (2016) · Geological (2018) · Industrial (2017) · Manufacturing (2009) · Mechanical (2016) · Mechatronics (2016/17) · Nuclear (2025) · Engineering Physics (2017) · Software (2019).

---

*Complete extraction of all 14 PEO Suggested Text Listings, April 2026. Source documents span 2004–2019 (originally published) with several updated as recently as August 2026 by PEO. "Latest edition" applies to every entry unless a specific edition is explicitly required (e.g., 18-Geol-B2's stereoscope + specific page range). Discipline groupings and subject codes (A-series/B-series) preserved exactly as PEO organizes them. This supersedes the earlier self-built PEO domain reading list — that one was a designed study plan; this is the authentic source material.*



### §6.C.COMPLETE: Military & Tactical Domain (Complete)


# MILITARY DOMAIN READING LIST — 12 BOOKS PER TOPIC
## Redji Jean Baptiste (Toussaint) — Companion to the Tactical & Military Mastery Domain
### 5 categories, 30 topics, 360 books

---

> **Scope and honesty note:** these lists draw from published military doctrine, professional military education (PME) reading lists (US Army/Marine Corps/Navy/Air Force Chief of Staff reading lists, Canadian Armed Forces professional development reading, NATO doctrine), classic strategic theory, and reputable technical/field manuals. As with the PEO expansion, confidence is highest for entries 1–6 per topic; entries 7–12 broaden coverage and should be verified for current edition/availability. Official field manuals (FM/ATP series, CAF publications) are the primary source for procedural/doctrinal topics — books listed here are the study/context layer around them, not a replacement for official doctrine where operational accuracy matters.

---

## SUMMARY

| Category | Topics | Total books |
|---|---|---|
| Military Foundation | 5 | 60 |
| Field & Operational Skills | 7 | 84 |
| Tactics & Planning | 6 | 72 |
| Technical | 6 | 72 |
| Leadership / Professional | 6 | 72 |
| **Total** | **30** | **360** |

**Cross-topic classics** (appear across 4+ topics — the genuine common backbone of military professional development regardless of specialty): *On War* (Clausewitz) · *The Art of Intelligence* (Crumpton) · *Extreme Ownership* (Willink & Babin) · Official doctrine (FM/MCDP/JP series, all free from official DoD/CAF sources) · *Open Source Intelligence Techniques* (Bazzell) · Sun Tzu's *The Art of War*.

**A note on official doctrine:** the free, official field manuals and joint publications (FM, MCDP, ADP, JP series from US DoD; equivalent CAF publications) referenced throughout are the actual authoritative source for procedure — they're listed first in most topics for a reason. The commercial books around them provide context, theory, and depth doctrine alone doesn't cover.
